# Corton parcel farming research register

Reviewed 2026-10-07; target season 2026.

**Current farmer identification remains incomplete: no parcel has confirmed current-operation evidence.**

All 728 mapped Corton parcels and 112 recorded legal-entity identifiers, reviewed for Tier 1 (commune edges, named-area audit, #461 history) and Tier 2: every holder searched in the official company register and the free filing index, 349 filings OCR-screened, schedules and lease recitals read, estate, importer and Winehog sources compared by named area. Holder-to-domaine links live in the shared holder table.

Of 728 mapped parcels, 387 have recorded rights and 341 have no matched right holder. All 112 holder groups were triaged. 262 parcels have holder-derived or independent-research leads, 3 have an exact-reference application or suspended application, 0 have an authorisation decision, 2 have co-sale leads through a later company holder, and 461 remain without a named candidate. These are mutually exclusive research categories, not farmer counts.

314 parcels have inventory records only, not individual source investigations. Historical application references can also lack matched rights.

82 parcels have reviewed company filings naming exact references with contribution, transfer, tenancy or purchase/lease mandate evidence. These do not confirm operation in the target season.

Candidate names below are hypotheses. Their basis ranges from estate context to a weak company-name or bottler connection. No confidence percentage is assigned; the stated evidence must be checked before accepting any relationship.

Geometry: 2026-06-01. Rights: 2025-01-01. Areas measure the intersection with the cru, not ownership shares or planted hectares. A mapped intersection may include a sliver or land whose farming applicability remains unknown.

Generated by `python scripts/build_grand_cru_research.py --cru corton`; use `--check` to verify. Edit [curation](curation.json), not this report. [Machine-readable parcel register](register.json) · [Evidence method and app behaviour](README.md).

## Named-area census

Parcels are grouped by the cadastral lieu-dit holding most of their geometry. For each named area the census compares land without a company record with the Corton holdings producers publish there. "Beyond company records" is a published area minus the land recorded to company records linked to that producer in the same named area: land the producer says it owns or farms, which the legal-entity files do not show. It is an area comparison only. It never places a holding on particular parcels, and a producer's published figure can be rounded, out of date or include leased land. A métayer farms land already recorded to its owner; a holding spread over several named areas cannot be split, so neither is counted. Disputed locations are listed as alternatives and excluded from numeric climat totals. `LA TOPPE AU VERT` has no reviewed crosswalk to La Toppe au Vert. `LE VILLAGE` has no reviewed crosswalk to Corton. `LES CARRIERES` has no reviewed crosswalk to Les Carrières. `BOIS DE CORTON`, `BOIS DE NAGET`, `EN CHAGNIARDS`, `LES PETITES LOLIERES` hold most of some parcels that only touch the cru edge; they are neighbouring lieux-dits, not Corton climats.

## Exact-reference administrative events

Reviewed farm-structure notices name the applicant, the previous operator and the cadastral references. A receipt of a complete application explicitly does not authorise cultivation; an authorisation is a dated decision, not proof of actual or current operation. References were read from the page image.

- **2021-11-16 — Thibaut Marion.** Application received; previous operator CPEF à Beaune. Parcels: AK 0036, AK 0159, AK 0164. Dossier 2021-180: Ladoix-Serrigny AK159, AK164 and AK36, 0.6457 ha; complete 4 November 2021. The receipt is not an authorisation. [Thibaut Marion application receipt, dossier 2021-180](https://www.prefectures-regions.gouv.fr/bourgogne-franche-comte/irecontenu/telechargement/94259/602873/file/recueil-bfc-2022-044-recueil-des-actes-administratifs-special.pdf#page=19).

## Parcel-specific company filings

Deed dates are separate from filing labels. Existing lease recitals, concurrent lease references and mandates are kept distinct; none proves current-season farming. See [the detailed reading and next source requests](filings.md).

- **1972 - Corton-Grancey GFA: founding contributions and Latour lease**. A 0046 (7933 m²), A 0047 (3967 m²), A 0049 (4252 m²), A 0050 (8145 m²), A 0051 (290 m²), B 0026 (6387 m²), B 0029 (9180 m²), B 0033 (8483 m²), B 0034 (7218 m²), B 0035 (1540 m²), B 0036 (3512 m²), C 0010 (5700 m²), C 0056 (3184 m²), D 0030 (5040 m²), D 0062 (11353 m²), N 0050 (14923 m²). The 1972 schedule, reproduced in the 2025 statutes, contributes undivided two-thirds of 13 of these parcels and B 33, B 34 and B 35 in full; all 16 match today’s references and areas. B 27 (1 a 13 ca, a garden) also matches but has no company record today. D 91 and D 97 are recited as merged into D 100 in 1972 and re-divided in 1983 (D 109 kept; D 108 exchanged for D 105 and D 106). The statutes recite that all contributed land is let to the Société Civile Domaine Louis Latour to 31 October 2031. [GFA du Domaine de Corton-Grancey: statutes updated 2025](https://actes.ccm2.net/acte/8a38b685-0c70-4954-ad15-eb55e25d155f#page=3).
- **2011-09-28 - Vignoble Latour: 2011 contribution of one-third shares**. A 0046 (7933 m²), A 0047 (3967 m²), A 0049 (4252 m²), A 0050 (8145 m²), A 0051 (290 m²), B 0026 (6387 m²), B 0029 (9180 m²), B 0036 (3512 m²), C 0010 (5700 m²), C 0056 (3184 m²), D 0030 (5040 m²), D 0062 (11353 m²), D 0105 (19 m²), D 0106 (31 m²), D 0109 (7825 m²), N 0050 (14923 m²). Maison Louis Latour contributes its undivided one-third of these 16 Corton parcels (and of D 23, recorded to another holder today, and one-sixth of I 141). All 16 match today’s references and areas; the Corton-Grancey GFA holds the other two-thirds. [VIGNOBLE LATOUR: statutes after the 28 September 2011 contribution](https://actes.ccm2.net/acte/b395bdda-43cc-467b-885d-c30ad373c068#page=1).
- **2011-09-28 - Latour Immeubles: 2011 contribution of the Aloxe-Corton cellar**. B 0028 (2428 m²). Maison Louis Latour contributes the cellar building on Les Perrières B 28 (24 a 28 ca), which lies inside the Corton outline. [LATOUR IMMEUBLES: statutes after the 28 September 2011 contribution](https://actes.ccm2.net/acte/10f5e549-5947-4c60-be23-9d6aa45aae75#page=1).
- **1974-11 - Belgrand-Latour GFA: founding contribution and Latour lease**. A 0042 (9031 m²), A 0055 (7482 m²), N 0009 (1445 m²), N 0010 (6293 m²). The founding contribution names these four parcels with areas matching today’s cadastre. The statutes recite that all contributed land is let to the Société Civile Domaine Louis Latour: the 1966 lease, then a new 25-year lease signed 6 December 2001, to 10 November 2026. [GFA Domaine Belgrand-Latour: statutes updated 17 May 2025](https://actes.ccm2.net/acte/4a921f53-c95f-439c-bc02-43f219827e10#page=5).
- **1975 - Marchal-Latour GFA: founding contribution and Latour lease**. A 0041 (7874 m²), A 0056 (11993 m²), A 0092 (1398 m²), D 0020 (3700 m²). The founding contribution names these four parcels with areas matching today’s cadastre. The statutes recite that all contributed land is let to the Société Civile Domaine Louis Latour: the 1966 lease, then a new 25-year lease of 7 December 2001, to 10 November 2026. [GFA Domaine Marchal-Latour: statutes updated 17 May 2025](https://actes.ccm2.net/acte/e075dff5-8ee1-4eb1-ac71-af0bd65cf103#page=2).
- **2005-12-15 - Rolland-Latour GFA: 2005 contribution and Latour lease**. A 0048 (9340 m²), B 0006 (19580 m²). The founding deed contributes A 48 (93 a 40 ca) and B 6 (1 ha 95 a 80 ca), both matching today’s cadastre, and recites their lease to the Société Civile Domaine Louis Latour by deed of 8 November 2001, for 11 November 2001 to 10 November 2026. [GFA Rolland-Latour: 15 December 2005 founding deed](https://actes.ccm2.net/acte/50ba4241-90a5-488b-b9e7-b334a719282f#page=19).
- **1967-09-23 - Chandon de Briailles: 1967 founding contributions**. A 0083 (1168 m²), D 0003 (960 m²), D 0005 (3505 m²), D 0027 (7435 m²), D 0032 (1385 m²), D 0047 (1765 m²), D 0055 (3233 m²), E 0098 (2742 m²), E 0105 (1230 m²). The 1967 statutes contribute these nine parcels with areas matching today’s cadastre. D 34 (54 a 15 ca) is also contributed but is recorded to another holder today; E 17 lies outside the cru. [Domaine Chandon de Briailles: 23 September 1967 founding statutes](https://actes.ccm2.net/acte/a4c8be0c-df33-4b35-be17-527bbbfc0b3f#page=9).
- **1988-10-25 - Ravaut GFA: 1988 founding contributions**. AH 0121 (168 m²), AH 0122 (973 m²), AH 0123 (1933 m²), AH 0124 (351 m²), D 0084 (2121 m²), D 0090 (952 m²), D 0101 (1190 m²). The 1988 statutes contribute these seven parcels, all matching today’s references and areas. AH 123 is split 10 a 00 ca planted for Corton-Charlemagne and 9 a 33 ca for red Corton. The GFA may only let its land; no tenant is named. [GFA du Domaine Ravaut Père et Fils: 25 October 1988 statutes](https://actes.ccm2.net/acte/7849c27c-d998-4597-b6fc-a49ecdae83a7#page=5).
- **2017 - Vincent Rapet GFA: 2017 contributions and tenant in place**. A 0069 (4565 m²), A 0088 (1250 m²), A 0121 (863 m²), N 0082 (3800 m²), AL 0308 (8973 m²). The founding deed contributes these five parcels with areas matching today’s cadastre. SAS du Domaine Rapet Père et Fils (319 775 417) declares itself the tenant in place of the contributed parcels; leases of 1985 (AL 308) and 1999 (A 69) are recited. [GFA du Domaine Vincent Rapet: 2017 founding deed](https://actes.ccm2.net/acte/509706b2-6590-45f0-9fc1-5b6ba2e423a6#page=9).
- **2009 - Marie Sordoillet GFV: 2009 contributions and lease**. A 0004 (4558 m²), A 0058 (3635 m²), A 0120 (5528 m²), B 0013 (5321 m²), D 0026 (1134 m²). The founding deed contributes these five parcels with areas matching today’s cadastre and recites their lease to Maurice Chapuis, agriculteur at Aloxe-Corton, personally: deed of 20 and 21 June 2001 renewing the lease for 25 years from 1 July 1995. [GFV Marie Sordoillet: 2009 founding deed](https://actes.ccm2.net/acte/33491379#page=8).
- **2024-01-24 - GFV Camila: 2024 contributions**. C 0061 (441 m²), C 0100 (6019 m²). Two contributors each bring an undivided half of Renardes C 61 (4 a 41 ca) and C 100 (60 a 19 ca); together 64 a 60 ca, the area Domaine Mallard publishes for its Corton Les Renardes. No lease is recited. [GFV Camila: 24 January 2024 founding deed](https://actes.ccm2.net/acte/4fa3606a-f47c-4f06-af51-cf1845535fa3#page=3).
- **2024-11-20 - GFV Bruno et Valérie Clavelier: 2024 contributions**. AK 0149 (205 m²), AK 0151 (811 m²), AK 0152 (2017 m²), AK 0155 (335 m²). The founding deed contributes these four Ladoix-Serrigny parcels (33 a 68 ca together), all matching today’s cadastre. The rights file records the GFV with bare ownership (N). [GFV Bruno et Valérie Clavelier: 20 November 2024 founding deed](https://actes.ccm2.net/acte/20fba88e-752b-4750-a5d7-0a5a23ac8afe#page=6).
- **2014-12 - GFV Saint Vincent Corton Les Maréchaudes: contribution and lease**. E 0117 (2880 m²). The founding deed contributes E 117 Les Maréchaudes (28 a 80 ca). A 2004 nine-year lease is to be ended and replaced by a 30-year long-term lease, retroactive to 1 September 2014, to one of the GFV’s managers personally. [GFV Saint Vincent Corton Les Maréchaudes: founding deed](https://actes.ccm2.net/acte/682cc768-a479-4982-94de-a921025010a0#page=4).
- **2002-08 - GFV Domaine Pierre et Anne-Marie Gille: Renardes C 51 and lease**. C 0051 (1625 m²). The 2002 deed lists Renardes C 51 (16 a 25 ca) among the GFV’s land and recites its lease, with Vosne-Romanée vines, to S.C.E.V. Domaine Anne Marie Gille under a 1996 lease running from 11 November 1995 to 10 November 2013. [GFV Domaine Pierre et Anne-Marie Gille: 2002 deed](https://actes.ccm2.net/acte/94d660c9-a82e-4e24-9cd7-24da1d15c748#page=3).
- **2023-06-21 - SCEA Corton Clos du Roi: 2023 contribution by Domaine de Montille**. D 0132 (2027 m²). Domaine de Montille contributes Clos du Roi D 132 (20 a 27 ca), divided from a parcel whose remainder of 21 a 49 ca it keeps (today’s D 0133, recorded to Montille), in exchange for 33,000 new shares. [SCEA Corton Clos du Roi: 21 June 2023 contribution deed](https://actes.ccm2.net/acte/9d6acf6d-1bc1-422d-8029-ac199d702955#page=1).
- **2024-07-24 - Mémoire de Vignes: Le Corton C 11 and Poisot-Piguet lease**. C 0011 (5700 m²). The parcel annex lists Le Corton C 11 (57 a 00 ca, Corton-Charlemagne). The statutes define an 18-year rural lease of the parcels, by deed of 24 July 2024, to SCEA du Domaine Poisot-Piguet (384 069 985). [MÉMOIRE DE VIGNES: statutes of 24 July 2024](https://actes.ccm2.net/acte/d1860966-223d-4e4e-841f-3e68dcc9a081#page=2).
- **2003-11-12 - GEROUK: purchase mandate for Renardes C 74**. C 0074 (3129 m²). The founding deed empowers a partner to buy Renardes C 74 (31 a 29 ca) on the GFA’s behalf for €390,000. The GFA holds C 0074 today; the purchase deed is not filed. [GEROUK GFA: 12 November 2003 founding deed (2019 copy)](https://actes.ccm2.net/acte/01c3688f-09eb-4b38-8bb9-dab576059ad7#page=37).
- **2022-03-29 - Fermière Viticole Remoissenet: 2022 merger asset list**. D 0125 (956 m²), AL 0336 (301 m²). The merger project transferring the company to Fermière Remoissenet SAS lists Les Bressandes D 125 (9 a 56 ca) and En Charlemagne AL 336 (3 a 01 ca), both matching today’s cadastre. [Fermière Viticole Remoissenet: merger project of 29 March 2022](https://actes.ccm2.net/acte/2bae0637-91bf-4311-ba7c-01b00ed80c0a#page=1).
- **2022-03-29 - Fermière Viticole Remoissenet: 2022 merger asset list (AL 328 and AL 332)**. AL 0328 (63 m²), AL 0332 (342 m²) (individual areas from the pinned cadastre; the deed recites only their combined area). The merger list gives En Charlemagne AL 328 and AL 332 together as 4 a 05 ca, equal to their combined cadastral area. [Fermière Viticole Remoissenet: merger project of 29 March 2022](https://actes.ccm2.net/acte/2bae0637-91bf-4311-ba7c-01b00ed80c0a#page=1).
- **2008-11-21 - GFA des Beaumonts: contributions and Maillard leases**. AK 0047 (1890 m²), AK 0049 (3982 m²). The founding deed contributes AK 47 (Corton grand cru, 18 a 90 ca) and AK 49 (39 a 82 ca) and recites both as let to S.A.R.L. Domaine Maillard Père et Fils: AK 47 under a 1982 lease prolonged to 31 December 2008, AK 49 under a 1996 lease to 10 November 2014. [GFA des Beaumonts: 21 November 2008 founding deed](https://actes.ccm2.net/acte/21ccf271-a107-4a80-826a-a78e3941087f#page=14).
- **2013-12 - GFA de la Maladière: Le Charlemagne A 14**. A 0014 (934 m²). The founding deed contributes Le Charlemagne A 14 (9 a 34 ca, Corton-Charlemagne). No lease is recited. [GFA de la Maladière: December 2013 founding deed](https://actes.ccm2.net/acte/70f2c8df-a737-401a-927d-1982c1fef343#page=5).
- **2012-06-29 - GFV Durst Corton-Charlemagne: En Charlemagne AL 181**. AL 0181 (610 m²). The company’s only asset is En Charlemagne AL 181 (6 a 10 ca), acquired by purchase. No lease is recited. [GFV Durst Corton-Charlemagne: 29 June 2012 deed](https://actes.ccm2.net/acte/ff0abb88-d09d-40d9-aa27-0b087d3637c0#page=4).
- **2010-12 - GFV Esprit 20 de Bourgogne: Hautes Mourottes AH 120 and lease mandate**. AH 0120 (7285 m²). The founding deed contributes Hautes Mourottes AH 120 (72 a 85 ca), described as coppice in the Ladoix area, and mandates a long-term lease of the contributed parcels to Vignobles Clemencet. [GFV Esprit 20 de Bourgogne: founding deed](https://actes.ccm2.net/acte/d6889836-7db4-4d91-9e07-4725a0bb919a#page=5).
- **2007-05-12 - GFV L’Empereur: En Charlemagne AL 320**. AL 0320 (2432 m²). The founding deed contributes En Charlemagne AL 320 (24 a 32 ca, 20 a 72 ca planted) and mandates a long-term rural lease to a named individual. [GFV L’Empereur: 12 May 2007 founding deed](https://actes.ccm2.net/acte/ee653e31-7c92-46c8-933b-645a83f1d59e#page=9).
- **1996-10-30 - GFV Vifoncier Charlemagne: En Charlemagne contributions**. AL 0130 (1190 m²), AL 0174 (644 m²), AL 0176 (236 m²). The founding deed contributes undivided rights in En Charlemagne AL 130 (11 a 90 ca), AL 174 (6 a 44 ca) and AL 176 (2 a 36 ca). No tenant is named. [GFV Vifoncier Charlemagne: 30 October 1996 founding deed](https://actes.ccm2.net/acte/9a2b23eb-79d0-4a2e-a22f-10dfc23e97b0#page=5).
- **2019-12-13 - GFV CHAPUIS: contributions and family lease**. A 0137 (767 m²), B 0075 (142 m²). The founding deed contributes A 137 (7 a 67 ca) and B 75 (1 a 42 ca), matching today’s cadastre, with B 72 and H 92, and recites a lease to a family member personally of 4 August 2018 for 18 years, 7 months and 16 days from 26 March 2018. [GFV CHAPUIS: 13 December 2019 founding deed](https://actes.ccm2.net/acte/ff2e83b6-a0c9-42c1-a195-540a781c8c6f#page=4).

## Independent research

Published vineyard research can name cadastral references or describe holdings. It is dated secondary evidence of ownership or production, cross-checked here against the recorded rights; it never establishes current farming. Area reconstructions are inferences; exact and near-area matches are distinguished. The source itself does not name those parcels. Dates follow an explicitly selected research source, not another supporting registry record.

- **Latour: Vigne au Saint plots 50 and 51** (N 0050, N 0051; critic-named-cadastral-reference). Winehog (2020) names La Vigne au Saint cadastre nos. 50 and 51, 1.9762 ha together, as Louis Latour’s; their cadastral areas (14,923 + 4,839 m²) sum exactly to that. The section letter is not printed; N is the only section of this climat. Sources: [Winehog: Louis Latour Corton Clos de la Vigne au Saint](https://winehog.org/terroir-insight-latour-vigne-saint-2-43680/).
- **Des Croix: Le Charlemagne plots 19 and 45** (A 0019, A 0045; critic-named-cadastral-reference). Winehog (2020) names Le Charlemagne cadastre no. 45 (0.1254 ha) and no. 19 (0.1233 ha) as Domaine des Croix’s; both match A 0045 and A 0019, recorded to Domaine des Croix, by number and area. Sources: [Winehog: Domaine des Croix Corton-Charlemagne](https://winehog.org/terroir-insight-domaine-des-croix-corton-charlemagne-2-44185/).
- **Des Croix: Grèves and Vigne au Saint areas** (B 0044, N 0086; critic-attribution-area-reconstructed). Winehog gives 0.5500 ha in Les Grèves and 0.2877 ha in La Vigne au Saint; they equal the cadastral areas of B 0044 and N 0086, both recorded to Domaine des Croix. Matched by area, not by printed number. Sources: [Winehog: Domaine des Croix Corton Les Grèves](https://winehog.org/terroir-insight-domaine-des-croix-corton-lesgreves-43706/), [Winehog: Domaine des Croix Corton La Vigne au Saint](https://winehog.org/terroir-insight-domaine-des-croix-corton-au-saint-43585/).
- **Méo-Camuzet: Vigne au Saint area** (N 0085; critic-attribution-area-reconstructed). Winehog (2020) says Méo-Camuzet owns 0.1983 ha in the west end of La Vigne au Saint, the cadastral area of N 0085, which is recorded to the GFA Saint Vincent La Vigne au Saint. No filing links that GFA to Méo-Camuzet. Sources: [Winehog: Domaine Méo-Camuzet Corton La Vigne au Saint](https://winehog.org/terroir-insight-domaine-meo-vigne-saint-2-2-43604/), [Domaine Méo-Camuzet: Corton La Vigne au Saint](https://www.meo-camuzet.com/en/the-wines/5/corton-la-vigne-au-saint).
- **Bouchard: Le Corton plots 8 and 9** (via lineage: C 0008 (retired) → C 0124, C 0125; C 0009 (retired) → C 0124, C 0125; critic-named-cadastral-reference). Winehog (2020) says Bouchard’s red Le Corton includes cadastre plots 8 and 9. C 0008 and C 0009, recorded to Maison Bouchard Père et Fils in 2019–2024, were merged into C 0121 (DFI 2024) and divided into today’s C 0124 and C 0125 (DFI 2025), which have no company record yet. The DFI lineage is partial in scope. Sources: [Winehog: Bouchard Père et Fils Le Corton](https://winehog.org/terroir-insight-bouchard-le-corton-43669/).
- **Chandon de Briailles: Clos du Roi and Maréchaudes parcels** (D 0003, D 0005, E 0098, E 0105; estate-area-exact-match). The estate’s parcel areas (Clos du Roi 0.3505 and 0.096 ha; Maréchaudes 0.2742 and 0.123 ha) equal the cadastral areas of D 0005, D 0003, E 0098 and E 0105, all recorded to the domaine’s company. The pages are undated. Sources: [Chandon de Briailles: Corton Clos du Roi](https://www.chandondebriailles.com/fr_FR/vin/corton-clos-du-roi), [Chandon de Briailles: Corton Les Maréchaudes](https://www.chandondebriailles.com/fr_FR/vin/corton-les-marechaudes).
- **Vougeraie: Clos du Roi area** (D 0011; estate-area-exact-match). Vougeraie states 0.497 ha for its Corton Clos du Roi (2024 page), the cadastral area of D 0011, recorded to Les Petits Fils de Pierre Ponnelle, which SFG La Vougeraie presides. Sources: [Domaine de la Vougeraie: Corton Le Clos du Roi 2024](https://www.domainedelavougeraie.com/fr/les-vins.r-693/grands-crus.r-703/corton-grand-cru.v-17126.html?id_millesime=57).
- **Mallard: Renardes area** (C 0061, C 0100; estate-area-exact-match). Mallard states 64 ares 60 of Corton Les Renardes; GFV Camila’s C 0061 (441 m²) and C 0100 (6,019 m²) sum exactly to it. Sources: [Domaine Mallard: Corton Les Renardes technical sheet](https://www.domaine-mallard.com/vins/images/pdf_appellations/CortonGrandCruLesRenardes.pdf).
- **Roumier: Corton-Charlemagne area** (AL 0192; estate-area-exact-match). Roumier states 0.2040 ha of Corton-Charlemagne in Pernand-Vergelesses, the cadastral area of AL 0192, recorded to GFA Clos de la Bussière. Sources: [Domaine Georges Roumier: Corton-Charlemagne](https://www.roumier.com/fr_FR/vin/corton-charlemagne).
- **Chanterêves: grape purchase from En Charlemagne no. 168** (AL 0168; critic-named-cadastral-reference). Winehog (2020) says Chanterêves buys grapes from En Charlemagne cadastre no. 168, 0.0433 ha, the cadastral area of AL 0168, which has no company record. A grape purchase names no owner or farmer, so no lead is created. Sources: [Winehog: Chanterêves Corton-Charlemagne](https://winehog.org/terroir-insight-domaine-chantereves-corton-charlemagne-2-44534/).
- **Louis Latour: 26.59 ha of Corton** (no cadastral reference; critic-holding-description). Winehog (2020) gives Louis Latour 26.59 ha on the Corton hill. Latour company records cover 12.64 ha of mapped overlap, 20.46 ha with the three family GFAs whose land is recited as let to the Société Civile Domaine Louis Latour; the figure may also include Corton-Charlemagne and other leased land. Sources: [Winehog: Louis Latour Corton Clos de la Vigne au Saint](https://winehog.org/terroir-insight-latour-vigne-saint-2-43680/), [Louis Latour: Château Corton Grancey](https://www.louislatour.com/en/maison-louis-latour/domaine-chateau-corton-grancey).
- **DRC: lease of part of Bonneau du Martray** (no cadastral reference; critic-holding-description). Winehog (2020) reports that Domaine de la Romanée-Conti took over cultivation of part of the Bonneau du Martray vineyard under a lease agreed in 2018. No parcels or area are named, so no parcel lead is created. Sources: [Winehog: Domaine de la Romanée-Conti Corton-Charlemagne](https://winehog.org/terroir-insight-domaine-romanee-conti-charlemagne-2-43710/).
- **Mallard: Corton plots by climat** (no cadastral reference; critic-holding-description). Winehog (2020) lists Mallard plots in Maréchaudes, Le Rognet, Renardes and Le Charlemagne. The company holds usufruct on Le Rognet and Le Charlemagne parcels; its Maréchaudes land has no linked company record. Sources: [Winehog: Domaine Michel Mallard Corton-Charlemagne](https://winehog.org/terroir-insight-domaine-michel-mallard-charlemagne-44757/).

## Rights history and parcel lineage

The legal-entity rights files for 1 January 2019–2025 were compared with the pinned 2025-01-01 snapshot, and Etalab cadastre vintages from 2017-07-06 with the 2026-06-01 geometry. 264 of 728 parcels had a recorded-rights change; 87 current references were not observed in the first obtained vintage, and 37 retired references overlapped the cru. Only company-type holders appear: a first record can be a purchase, a transfer from private owners into a family company, or a new reference after a split. Continuity is proved only by an unchanged SIREN. None of this is farming evidence. [Full yearly records and lineage](rights-history.json), rebuilt by `python scripts/build_grand_cru_rights_history.py --cru corton`.

**Retired references and their current successors**

A successor is accepted when it first appears in the vintage right after the retired reference and lies almost entirely inside it. Other overlaps, such as boundary slivers or parcels that already existed, are rejected candidates and never carry evidence. The rule is spatial, not a documented division act.

## Sale and exchange deeds

[Cerema DVF+ open-data, Bourgogne-Franche-Comté](https://www.data.gouv.fr/datasets/dvf-open-data) (2014-01-07 to 2025-12-22) list registered transfers for a fee by deed, without buyer or seller. Prices are not kept. A later annual company record can follow an intervening contribution or another transfer, so it does not identify the deed buyer. A single-disposition co-sale with parcels later recorded to a company is only a lead to investigate those transfers. Exchanges move parcels in both directions and give no lead. Rebuilt by `python scripts/build_grand_cru_sale_records.py --cru corton`.

DFI validation dates do not extend the coverage of sales or notices. Original dates, references and scope stay with each record. Historical context never transfers rights, sale parties or farming.

- Côte-d'Or 2007, 2009, 2012 and 2014: no bulletin obtained (no archived listing and no dated capture)
- Côte-d'Or 2004–2006, 2008, 2010, 2011, 2013 and 2015: only the archived captures in the acquisition report; annual completeness unknown. The 2013 and 2015 listings link 96 bulletins with no capture of the listed URL; 17 listed Côte-d'Or bulletins are probably obtained as same-filename captures whose cover year matches (see the Common Crawl report)
- Departmental 2021–2026 publications outside the existing index
- Regional publications before 2019 and any notice outside the indexed corpus
- Before 2004: not searched

## Source log

Publication/document dates and vintage seasons are separate fields in the curation. An undated page, recent upload or review date does not establish operation in the target season.

- **dgfip-current** — [DGFiP department 21 legal-entity rights, 2025-01-01](https://data.economie.gouv.fr/api/explore/v2.1/catalog/datasets/fichiers-des-locaux-et-des-parcelles-des-personnes-morales/attachments/fichier_des_parcelles_situation_2025_dpts_01_a_56_zip). registry-dataset; read; document date 2025-01-01. Exact current references and every holder/right code retained. Rights are not farming.
- **dgfip-history** — [DGFiP available annual legal-entity rights](https://www.data.gouv.fr/datasets/fichiers-des-locaux-et-des-parcelles-des-personnes-morales). registry-dataset; read; document date not established. Pinned annual source members and original-reference dates are in rights-history.json and the shared inventory.
- **cadastre-history** — [Etalab available commune geometry vintages](https://files.data.gouv.fr/cadastre/etalab-cadastre/). geometry; read; document date not established. Full current geometry is preserved. Earlier observations support separately labelled spatial inference.
- **dvf-sales** — [Cerema DVF+ open-data, Bourgogne-Franche-Comté](https://www.data.gouv.fr/datasets/dvf-open-data). registry-dataset; read; document date not established. Dated deed references, without prices or parties. Coverage is audited independently of DFI.
- **girard-2021** — [EARL Domaine Philippe Girard application receipt, dossier 2021-122](https://www.prefectures-regions.gouv.fr/bourgogne-franche-comte/irecontenu/telechargement/89554/574828/file/recueil-bfc-2021-146-recueil-des-actes-administratifs-special.pdf#page=37). government-event; read; document date 2021-07-26. The receipt prints A139 under Chambolle-Musigny; its Aloxe-Corton references are L94, L24, L25, H59, E32 and E19. Aloxe-Corton A0139 is not named, so no event is assigned to this cru.
- **thibaut-marion-2021** — [Thibaut Marion application receipt, dossier 2021-180](https://www.prefectures-regions.gouv.fr/bourgogne-franche-comte/irecontenu/telechargement/94259/602873/file/recueil-bfc-2022-044-recueil-des-actes-administratifs-special.pdf#page=19). government-event; read; document date 2021-11-16. The receipt prints Ladoix-Serrigny AK159, AK164 and AK36 (0.6457 ha), previous operator CPEF à Beaune; filed and complete 4 November 2021. All three are current Corton parcels in Ladoix-Serrigny (21606). The receipt alone does not authorise cultivation.
- **wh-gaunoux** — [Winehog: Domaine Michel Gaunoux Corton Renardes](https://winehog.org/terroir-insight-domaine-michel-gaunoux-renardes-44255/). critic-research; read; document date 2020-12-02. Free part states that Gaunoux owns 0.63 ha in the core of Corton Renardes. No parcel number; the rest of the article is for subscribers.
- **wh-croix-greves** — [Winehog: Domaine des Croix Corton Les Grèves](https://winehog.org/terroir-insight-domaine-des-croix-corton-lesgreves-43706/). critic-research; read; document date 2020-11-21. States that Domaine des Croix owns 0.5500 ha in the middle of Les Grèves.
- **wh-croix-vas** — [Winehog: Domaine des Croix Corton La Vigne au Saint](https://winehog.org/terroir-insight-domaine-des-croix-corton-au-saint-43585/). critic-research; read; document date 2020-10-15. States that Domaine des Croix owns 0.2877 ha in the western end of La Vigne au Saint.
- **wh-croix-cc** — [Winehog: Domaine des Croix Corton-Charlemagne](https://winehog.org/terroir-insight-domaine-des-croix-corton-charlemagne-2-44185/). critic-research; read; document date 2020-11-22. States two Le Charlemagne plots owned by the domaine: cadastre no. 45 (0.1254 ha) and no. 19 (0.1233 ha).
- **wh-montille-cdr** — [Winehog: Domaine de Montille Corton Clos du Roi](https://winehog.org/terroir-insight-domaine-montille-cls-du-roi-43708/). critic-research; read; document date 2020-10-30. States that Montille acquired its Clos du Roi in 2005, buying part of the Thomas Moillard estate with Domaine Dujac.
- **wh-montille-pougets** — [Winehog: Domaine de Montille Corton Pougets](https://winehog.org/terroir-insight-domaine-montille-pougets-43616/). critic-research; read; document date 2020-10-21. States that Montille owns 1.02 ha in the eastern end of Les Pougets, acquired in 2004 and since regrafted or replanted with chardonnay.

States that Louis Latour owns 26.59 ha of Corton, and two Vigne au Saint plots, cadastre nos. 50 and 51, totalling 1.9762 ha.

S2V holds 99 of the SCI’s 100 shares. The SCI owns two vineyard parcels, Pernand “Les Plantes” and Corton “Les Chaumes”, both under farm leases to S2V. No cadastral references are printed.
- **corton-clos-du-roi-2023** — [SCEA Corton Clos du Roi: 21 June 2023 contribution deed](https://actes.ccm2.net/acte/9d6acf6d-1bc1-422d-8029-ac199d702955#page=1). company-filing; read; document date 2023-06-21. Domaine de Montille (483134516) contributes Aloxe-Corton Le Clos du Roi D 132 (20 a 27 ca), split from a parcel whose remainder (21 a 49 ca) it keeps, in exchange for 33,000 new shares of SCEA Corton Clos du Roi (953306917).
- **corton-grancey-statutes** — [GFA du Domaine de Corton-Grancey: statutes updated 2025](https://actes.ccm2.net/acte/8a38b685-0c70-4954-ad15-eb55e25d155f#page=3). company-filing; read; document date 1972. The statutes reproduce the 1972 founding contributions: undivided two-thirds of Aloxe-Corton B 26, B 29, B 36, C 10, C 56, D 30, D 62, A 46, A 47, A 49, A 50, A 51 and N 50, and B 33, B 34 and B 35 in full, each with its area. They recite that all contributed land was let to the Société Civile Domaine Louis Latour (Château Corton-Grancey) under a 26 March 1966 lease, renewed in 1998 and again on 3 June 2013 to 31 October 2031.

## Remaining evidence and access gaps

- Tier 2 screened 349 free filings (about 8,400 PDF pages) of 105 companies by OCR and read the schedules and lease recitals it cites from page images or text layers. It is not an exhaustive reading of every corporate archive. Paid land-registry (SPF) copies and outreach remain Tier 3.
- Fourteen operating-company filings without contribution or merger content were deliberately not OCR-screened.
- Private individuals are absent from the legal-entity file: 341 parcels have no matched company record. Published holdings compare whole named-area totals and do not assign those parcels.
- No filing reviewed is a signed current lease plus evidence of actual operation. Lease recitals in statutes (Latour GFAs to 2026 and 2031, Les Chagnots, Mémoire de Vignes, Vincent Rapet) and mandates (Esprit 20, L’Empereur) do not prove current farming.
- Several recited leases have lapsed on their stated terms (Beaumonts 2008/2014, Gille 2013, Sordoillet 2020) and renewals are not recited; the Belgrand-, Marchal- and Rolland-Latour leases end on 10 November 2026.
- Five provisional identifiers remain without a SIREN: SC Dom Hippolyte Thevenot, GFA Dom Emile Voarick, SCI des Bressandes, SCI Royland and the Beaune water syndicate; SCI des Domaines Dupray’s name matches a separately recorded SIREN in this cru.
- The free filing index mislabels two Latour filings (each appears under the other company); their content was read and attributed by the company named in the document.
- Winehog’s subscriber-only parcel details were not accessed; its free parts were read. Estate pages for Domaine Parent (HTTP 429), Château Corton C, Pavelot, Tollot-Beaut, Chevalier and Dublère were unavailable or gave no Corton area.
- Bouchard’s 2026 partial asset contribution does not itemise which vineyards moved to the new Bouchard Père et Fils company; the 2025 rights file predates it.
- Source boundaries, failed notice downloads and unsearched intervals are reported in the history coverage; none means no historical record.
- Current farming remains unconfirmed for every parcel.

For each proposed farmer, request a dated, shareable confirmation naming the exact cadastral IDs, whole/partial scope, season and operating entity; check any cadastral splits or merges. An appropriately shared CVI extract or parcel-specific lease plus evidence of actual operation may resolve the join. Do not publish private records without permission. No outreach has been sent.
